import numpy as np

# Filter the DataFrame for record_id 101
df_101 = df[df['record_id'] == 101]

# Define the known annotation-code list
known_annotations = ['+', 'N', '~', '|', 'Q', 'A', 'V', '/', 'x', 'F', 'j', 'L', 'a', 'R', 'J', '[', '!', ']', 'E', 'S', '"', 'f', 'e']

# Create 10-second bins

# Initialize lists to store bin indices and annotated-row rates
bin_indices = []
annotated_row_rates = []

# Iterate over the bins
for i in range(len(bins) - 1):
    # Filter the DataFrame for the current bin
    bin_df = df_101[(df_101['time_s'] >= bins[i]) & (df_101['time_s'] < bins[i + 1])]
    
    # Check if the bin is not empty
    if not bin_df.empty:
        # Compute the annotated-row rate for the current bin
        annotated_rows = bin_df[bin_df['annotation'].isin(known_annotations)]
        annotated_row_rate = len(annotated_rows) / len(bin_df)
        
        # Append the bin index and annotated-row rate to the lists
        bin_indices.append(i)
        annotated_row_rates.append(annotated_row_rate)

# Compute the Pearson correlation between bin index and annotated-row rate
correlation = np.corrcoef(bin_indices, annotated_row_rates)[0, 1]

result = correlation
